---
# PLAN — Live Armenian → English transcription

Target machine: **MacBook, Apple Silicon, 8 GB RAM, macOS.** Everything runs locally.

## Goal
Capture mic + system audio at the same time, transcribe Armenian, translate to English, label speakers
(`Me` for mic, `Speaker N` for the call side, stable for the session), and print each segment within
**5 s of the end of the phrase**.

## Hard constraints that drive the design
- **8 GB RAM** shared by OS, call app, and all models. Budget roughly ≤ 4.5 GB for models in total.
- **≤ 5 s latency** from end of phrase to printed line. Budget: VAD end-of-speech detection ~0.5 s, ASR ≤ 2.5 s,
  translation ≤ 0.5 s, speaker embedding ≤ 0.2 s, headroom ~1 s.
- **Armenian is low-resource.** Zero-shot Whisper large-v3 is poor on Armenian; fine-tuned checkpoints are much better.
  Model choice must be decided by *measurement on this Mac*, not by model cards.

## Modules (inputs → outputs)

| Module | File | In | Out |
|---|---|---|---|
| Device listing / selection | `audio/devices.py` | CLI flags | device indices for mic and loopback |
| Capture | `audio/capture.py` | two input devices | per-source stream of 16 kHz mono float32 chunks, tagged `mic` / `system` |
| Segmenter (VAD) | `segmenter.py` | chunk stream for one source | `Segment(source, audio, t_start, t_end)` when speech ends (silence ≥ ~500 ms) or hits max length (~8 s) |
| ASR | `asr/` (interface + backends) | `Segment.audio` | Armenian text |
| Translation | `translate.py` | Armenian text | English text |
| Speakers | `speakers.py` | `Segment` | label: `Me` for mic; for system: embedding → online clustering → `Speaker N` |
| Output | `output.py` | time, label, HY, EN, measured latency | terminal lines |
| Pipeline | `main.py` | everything above | wires threads + queues together |
| Evaluation | `eval/` | WAV files + reference text | CER/WER per model, latency stats |

Threading: one capture callback per device → one VAD per source → **one shared processing worker** (models loaded once;
8 GB can't hold two copies). Segments processed in order of `t_end`.

## Model candidates (to benchmark, not yet decided)

**ASR**
1. `BuzzASR/armenian` — Whisper large-v3 full fine-tune, card reports FLEURS WER 11.9 %, CV WER 2.4 %. ~3 GB in fp16.
   Risk: memory and latency on 8 GB; custom tokenizer means it must run via `transformers` (not mlx-whisper).
2. `Chillarmo/whisper-large-v3-turbo-armenian` — turbo fine-tune (4 decoder layers → much faster decoding), ~1.6 GB fp16.
   No reliable published WER found yet → we measure it.
3. `facebook/mms-1b-all` + `hye` adapter — CTC, fast, no hallucination loops; likely less accurate. Baseline.
4. `openai/whisper-large-v3-turbo` zero-shot — reference point only, to show why fine-tunes are needed.

**Translation**: `facebook/nllb-200-distilled-600M` converted to CTranslate2 int8 (~0.6 GB, fast on CPU),
`hye_Armn → eng_Latn`. Alternative if quality is poor: NLLB 1.3B distilled int8 (~1.4 GB) — measure memory/latency first.
Note: NLLB license is CC-BY-NC (fine for this challenge, state it in README).

**Speaker embeddings**: `speechbrain/spkrec-ecapa-voxceleb` (192-d) or a WeSpeaker ONNX model.
Clustering: online centroid clustering with a cosine-similarity threshold, tuned on recordings.

**VAD**: Silero VAD (small, CPU, reliable).

## Risks
- **Memory pressure** on 8 GB → swapping → latency blows up. Mitigation: measure RSS, prefer turbo if large-v3 doesn't fit.
- **Whisper hallucinations** on silence / noise (repeated phrases, invented text). Mitigation: only send VAD speech;
  `no_repeat_ngram_size`; drop segments < 0.4 s.
- **Long utterances** → latency > 5 s. Mitigation: force-cut at ~8 s.
- **Both sides talking at once** → queue builds up. Mitigation: measure; print queue delay; process oldest first.
- **Short call-side segments** give poor speaker embeddings. Open question below.
- **BlackHole routing**: user must still hear the call → Multi-Output Device (speakers + BlackHole).
- Call apps apply their own noise suppression / compression → audio differs from clean recordings.

## How we test
- `TESTS.md`: requirement-driven test cases and QA checklist, written as we go.
- Unit tests (pytest) for pure logic: segmenter on synthetic audio, speaker clustering on synthetic embeddings,
  output formatting, latency accounting.
- **Evaluation set** (`eval/data/`, not committed): ~10–20 short Armenian clips recorded by you, plus 2–3 voices
  for speaker tests, each with the correct transcript. `eval/run_asr.py` reports CER/WER and latency per model.
- End-to-end rehearsal: a real call with 2 people on the call side before the interview.

## Build order (walking skeleton first)
0. Env setup + BlackHole + `--list-devices`. Repo skeleton, PLAN/DECISIONS/TESTS files.
1. **Skeleton**: WAV file or mic → VAD → ASR (cheapest candidate) → NLLB → terminal, with latency printed.
2. **Measure**: record eval set, benchmark ASR candidates on your Mac → pick model (log in DECISIONS.md).
3. Second source: mic + BlackHole together, `Me` label.
4. Speaker clustering on system side, tune threshold on recordings.
5. Hardening (weakest link first), README, limitations.

## Open questions for you
1. Can you record the eval set (I'll give you a script + a list of sentences), ideally with 2–3 other people?
2. Short call-side segments (< ~1 s) where the voice embedding is unreliable: label them `Speaker ?` (honest)
   or assign to the closest existing speaker (looks cleaner but can be wrong)?
3. Python version on your Mac (`python3 --version`)? I'd target 3.11.
```

"""Cut a continuous 16 kHz stream into utterances using a voice-activity probability per frame.

Pure logic: the VAD is passed in as a function, so this module is testable without any model.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

from collections import deque
from typing import Callable

import numpy as np

from livetr import SAMPLE_RATE
from livetr.types import Segment

VadFn = Callable[[np.ndarray], float]  # frame (float32, frame_size samples) -> speech probability 0..1


class Segmenter:
    def __init__(
        self,
        source: str,
        vad: VadFn,
        *,
        frame_size: int = 512,          # Silero VAD requires exactly 512 samples at 16 kHz (32 ms)
        threshold: float = 0.5,         # prob >= threshold starts speech
        neg_threshold: float | None = None,  # prob < neg_threshold counts as silence (hysteresis)
        min_silence_ms: int = 500,      # pause that ends an utterance
        speech_pad_ms: int = 160,       # audio kept before speech start and after speech end
        min_speech_ms: int = 400,       # shorter utterances are dropped (clicks, coughs)
        max_segment_s: float = 15.0,    # force a cut so ASR time stays bounded (see DECISIONS.md)
    ):
        self.source = source
        self.vad = vad
        self.frame_size = frame_size
        self.frame_s = frame_size / SAMPLE_RATE
        self.threshold = threshold
        self.neg_threshold = threshold - 0.15 if neg_threshold is None else neg_threshold
        self.min_silence_frames = max(1, round(min_silence_ms / 1000 / self.frame_s))
        self.pad_frames = round(speech_pad_ms / 1000 / self.frame_s)
        self.min_speech_frames = round(min_speech_ms / 1000 / self.frame_s)
        self.max_frames = round(max_segment_s / self.frame_s)

        self.dropped_short = 0  # counter, reported in --stats
        self._buf = np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float32)
        self._pre: deque[np.ndarray] = deque(maxlen=max(self.pad_frames, 1))
        self._reset_segment()

    def _reset_segment(self) -> None:
        self._in_speech = False
        self._frames: list[np.ndarray] = []
        self._start_wall = 0.0
        self._speech_frames = 0
        self._silence_run = 0
        self._last_speech_idx = -1
        self._last_speech_end = 0.0

    def push(self, audio: np.ndarray, end_wall: float) -> list[Segment]:
        """Feed audio of any length. `end_wall` is the monotonic time of its last sample."""
        if audio.dtype != np.float32 or audio.ndim != 1:
            raise ValueError(f"expected 1-D float32 audio, got {audio.dtype} with shape {audio.shape}")
        self._buf = np.concatenate([self._buf, audio])
        n_frames = len(self._buf) // self.frame_size
        leftover = len(self._buf) - n_frames * self.frame_size
        out: list[Segment] = []
        for i in range(n_frames):
            frame = self._buf[i * self.frame_size:(i + 1) * self.frame_size]
            samples_after = (n_frames - i - 1) * self.frame_size + leftover
            frame_end = end_wall - samples_after / SAMPLE_RATE
            seg = self._step(frame, frame_end)
            if seg is not None:
                out.append(seg)
        self._buf = self._buf[n_frames * self.frame_size:].copy()
        return out

    def flush(self) -> list[Segment]:
        """End of stream: emit whatever speech is pending."""
        if not self._in_speech:
            return []
        seg = self._emit(forced=False)
        return [seg] if seg is not None else []

    def _step(self, frame: np.ndarray, frame_end: float) -> Segment | None:
        prob = self.vad(frame)
        if not self._in_speech:
            if prob >= self.threshold:
                self._in_speech = True
                self._frames = list(self._pre) + [frame]
                self._start_wall = frame_end - len(self._frames) * self.frame_s
                self._speech_frames = 1
                self._silence_run = 0
                self._last_speech_idx = len(self._frames) - 1
                self._last_speech_end = frame_end
                self._pre.clear()
            else:
                self._pre.append(frame)
            return None

        self._frames.append(frame)
        if prob >= self.neg_threshold:
            self._speech_frames += 1
            self._silence_run = 0
            self._last_speech_idx = len(self._frames) - 1
            self._last_speech_end = frame_end
        else:
            self._silence_run += 1

        if self._silence_run >= self.min_silence_frames:
            trailing = self._frames[self._last_speech_idx + 1:]
            seg = self._emit(forced=False)
            self._pre.extend(trailing)  # silence after the cut can pre-roll the next utterance
            return seg
        if len(self._frames) >= self.max_frames:
            # Cut at the current frame: the phrase is not over, but waiting longer breaks the latency budget.
            self._last_speech_idx = len(self._frames) - 1
            self._last_speech_end = frame_end
            seg = self._emit(forced=True)
            self._in_speech = True  # speech continues into the next segment
            self._start_wall = frame_end
            return seg
        return None

    def _emit(self, forced: bool) -> Segment | None:
        end_idx = self._last_speech_idx + 1 + (0 if forced else self.pad_frames)
        frames = self._frames[:end_idx]
        speech_frames = self._speech_frames
        start_wall, end_wall = self._start_wall, self._last_speech_end
        self._reset_segment()
        if speech_frames < self.min_speech_frames:
            self.dropped_short += 1
            return None
        return Segment(
            source=self.source,
            audio=np.concatenate(frames).astype(np.float32),
            start_wall=start_wall,
            end_wall=end_wall,
            forced_cut=forced,
        )


class SileroVAD:
    """Silero VAD wrapper. Stateful: use one instance per audio source."""

    def __init__(self) -> None:
        import torch
        from silero_vad import load_silero_vad

        self._torch = torch
        self._model = load_silero_vad()

    def __call__(self, frame: np.ndarray) -> float:
        with self._torch.no_grad():
            return float(self._model(self._torch.from_numpy(frame), SAMPLE_RATE).item())
"""Wires sources -> converter -> VAD segmenter -> shared queue -> one worker (ASR, speaker, translation) -> printer.

Threads:
  - one PortAudio callback per device (inside sounddevice) puts raw blocks on the source's queue
  - one `source loop` thread per source: down-mix, resample, VAD, push finished segments to `segments`
  - one `worker` thread: holds the models and processes segments one at a time (models load once: 8 GB RAM)
Any exception in a thread stops the whole program with the traceback (fail loudly).
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import queue
import sys
import threading
import time
import traceback
from typing import Callable, Protocol

import numpy as np

from livetr.audio.capture import Converter
from livetr.output import Printer, Stats
from livetr.segmenter import Segmenter
from livetr.types import Result, Segment


class Asr(Protocol):
    def transcribe(self, audio: np.ndarray) -> str: ...


class Translator(Protocol):
    def translate(self, text: str) -> str: ...


class Labeler(Protocol):
    def label(self, segment: Segment) -> str: ...


class Processor:
    """Turns one segment into one printed result. No threads here, so it can be tested directly."""

    def __init__(self, asr: Asr, translator: Translator, labeler: Labeler, printer: Printer, stats: Stats,
                 clock: Callable[[], float] = time.monotonic):
        self.asr = asr
        self.translator = translator
        self.labeler = labeler
        self.printer = printer
        self.stats = stats
        self.clock = clock

    def process(self, segment: Segment) -> Result | None:
        timings: dict[str, float] = {}
        t = self.clock()
        timings["wait"] = max(0.0, t - segment.end_wall)

        hy = self.asr.transcribe(segment.audio).strip()
        t2 = self.clock()
        timings["asr"] = t2 - t
        if not hy:
            self.stats.empty_asr += 1
            return None

        # Speaker after ASR: segments with no recognised speech must not shape the speaker clusters.
        speaker = self.labeler.label(segment)
        t3 = self.clock()
        timings["spk"] = t3 - t2

        en = self.translator.translate(hy)
        t4 = self.clock()
        timings["mt"] = t4 - t3

        result = Result(segment=segment, speaker=speaker, hy=hy, en=en, printed_wall=t4, timings=timings)
        self.printer.show(result)
        self.stats.add(result)
        return result


class Pipeline:
    def __init__(self, sources: list, vad_factory: Callable[[], Callable[[np.ndarray], float]],
                 processor: Processor, stats: Stats, segmenter_kwargs: dict | None = None):
        self.sources = sources
        self.processor = processor
        self.stats = stats
        self.segments: queue.Queue[Segment] = queue.Queue()
        self.stop_event = threading.Event()
        self.error: BaseException | None = None
        self.segmenters = {s.name: Segmenter(s.name, vad_factory(), **(segmenter_kwargs or {})) for s in sources}
        self._threads: list[threading.Thread] = []

    def _guard(self, fn: Callable[[], None]) -> Callable[[], None]:
        def run() -> None:
            try:
                fn()
            except BaseException as e:  # noqa: BLE001 - re-raised in the main thread
                self.error = e
                traceback.print_exc()
                self.stop_event.set()
        return run

    def _source_loop(self, source) -> None:
        convert = Converter(source.sample_rate)
        seg = self.segmenters[source.name]
        while not self.stop_event.is_set():
            try:
                block = source.blocks.get(timeout=0.1)
            except queue.Empty:
                if getattr(source, "done", None) is not None and source.done.is_set():
                    for s in seg.flush():
                        self.segments.put(s)
                    return
                continue
            audio = convert(block.audio)
            if len(audio):
                for s in seg.push(audio, block.end_wall):
                    self.segments.put(s)

    def _worker(self) -> None:
        while not self.stop_event.is_set():
            try:
                segment = self.segments.get(timeout=0.1)
            except queue.Empty:
                continue
            self.processor.process(segment)
            self.segments.task_done()

    def start(self) -> None:
        for src in self.sources:
            t = threading.Thread(target=self._guard(lambda s=src: self._source_loop(s)),
                                 name=f"src-{src.name}", daemon=True)
            self._threads.append(t)
        self._threads.append(threading.Thread(target=self._guard(self._worker), name="worker", daemon=True))
        for t in self._threads:
            t.start()
        for src in self.sources:
            src.start()

    def files_finished(self) -> bool:
        """True when every source is a file that has been fully played and processed."""
        if not all(getattr(s, "done", None) is not None and s.done.is_set() for s in self.sources):
            return False
        source_threads = [t for t in self._threads if t.name.startswith("src-")]
        return all(not t.is_alive() for t in source_threads) and self.segments.unfinished_tasks == 0

    def run_until_stopped(self) -> None:
        try:
            while not self.stop_event.is_set():
                if self.files_finished():
                    break
                time.sleep(0.2)
        except KeyboardInterrupt:
            print("\nstopping…", file=sys.stderr)
        finally:
            self.stop_event.set()
            for src in self.sources:
                src.stop()
            self.stats.dropped_short = sum(s.dropped_short for s in self.segmenters.values())
            self.stats.overflows = sum(s.overflows for s in self.sources)
        if self.error is not None:
            raise SystemExit(f"stopped because of an error in a pipeline thread: {self.error!r}")
```

"""Command line entry point: python -m livetr [options]"""
from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import os
import sys
import time

import numpy as np

from livetr.asr import MODELS

DEFAULT_ASR = "nemo-hy"  # most accurate and fastest on FLEURS; see DECISIONS.md (2026-10-23)


def build_parser() -> argparse.ArgumentParser:
    p = argparse.ArgumentParser(
        prog="python -m livetr",
        description="Live Armenian transcription + English translation with speaker labels (local models only).",
    )
    g = p.add_argument_group("audio")
    g.add_argument("--list-devices", action="store_true", help="print input devices and exit")
    g.add_argument("--meter", action="store_true", help="show input levels for both sources (setup check) and exit")
    g.add_argument("--mic", default=None, help="microphone: device index or part of its name (default: system default input)")
    g.add_argument("--system", default="BlackHole", help="system-audio loopback device (default: 'BlackHole')")
    g.add_argument("--no-mic", action="store_true", help="do not capture the microphone")
    g.add_argument("--no-system", action="store_true", help="do not capture system audio")
    g.add_argument("--file", default=None, help="play a 16-bit WAV file in real time instead of live devices")
    g.add_argument("--file-source", choices=["mic", "system"], default="system", help="treat --file as this source")

    m = p.add_argument_group("models")
    m.add_argument("--asr", choices=sorted(MODELS), default=DEFAULT_ASR, help=f"ASR model (default {DEFAULT_ASR})")
    m.add_argument("--device", choices=["auto", "mps", "cpu"], default="cpu",
                   help="device for ASR (default cpu: measured fastest for this small model)")
    m.add_argument("--nllb-dir", default="models/nllb-200-distilled-1.3B-ct2-int8",
                   help="translation model (fallback if too slow: models/nllb-200-distilled-600M-ct2-int8)")
    m.add_argument("--allow-download", action="store_true",
                   help="allow Hugging Face downloads at runtime (default: offline, models must be downloaded first)")

    s = p.add_argument_group("segmentation and speakers")
    s.add_argument("--vad-threshold", type=float, default=0.5)
    s.add_argument("--min-silence-ms", type=int, default=500)
    s.add_argument("--max-segment-s", type=float, default=15.0)
    s.add_argument("--speaker-threshold", type=float, default=0.45, help="cosine similarity for 'same speaker'")
    s.add_argument("--max-speakers", type=int, default=8)

    o = p.add_argument_group("output")
    o.add_argument("--no-color", action="store_true")
    o.add_argument("--quiet-timings", action="store_true", help="hide per-line latency details")
    return p


def run_meter(sources: list, seconds: float = 15.0) -> None:
    from livetr.audio.capture import Converter

    converters = {s.name: Converter(s.sample_rate) for s in sources}
    levels = {s.name: -90.0 for s in sources}
    for s in sources:
        s.start()
    print("Speak, and play some audio on the computer. Ctrl-C to stop.\n")
    end = time.monotonic() + seconds
    try:
        while time.monotonic() < end:
            for s in sources:
                while not s.blocks.empty():
                    a = converters[s.name](s.blocks.get().audio)
                    if len(a):
                        rms = float(np.sqrt(np.mean(a ** 2)) + 1e-9)
                        levels[s.name] = max(20 * np.log10(rms), -90.0)
            line = "   ".join(f"{n:<6} {'█' * int((lv + 60) / 2.5) if lv > -60 else '':<24} {lv:6.1f} dB"
                             for n, lv in levels.items())
            print("\r" + line, end="", flush=True)
            time.sleep(0.1)
    except KeyboardInterrupt:
        pass
    finally:
        for s in sources:
            s.stop()
        print()


def main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> None:
    args = build_parser().parse_args(argv)
    if not args.allow_download:
        # Local-only guarantee: no network at runtime. Models come from scripts/download_models.py.
        os.environ["HF_HUB_OFFLINE"] = "1"
        os.environ["TRANSFORMERS_OFFLINE"] = "1"

    from livetr.audio import devices as dev
    from livetr.types import MIC, SYSTEM

    if args.list_devices:
        print(dev.format_input_devices(dev.query_devices()))
        return

    # ---- sources -------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    sources = []
    if args.file:
        from livetr.audio.capture import FileSource

        sources.append(FileSource(MIC if args.file_source == "mic" else SYSTEM, args.file))
    else:
        from livetr.audio.capture import DeviceSource

        devices = dev.query_devices()
        try:
            mic_idx = None if args.no_mic else (
                dev.resolve_input_device(args.mic, devices) if args.mic else dev.default_input_index())
            sys_idx = None if args.no_system else dev.resolve_input_device(args.system, devices)
        except dev.DeviceError as e:
            sys.exit(f"error: {e}")
        if mic_idx is not None and mic_idx == sys_idx:
            sys.exit("error: mic and system audio are the same device. Pass --mic with your real microphone.")
        if mic_idx is not None:
            sources.append(DeviceSource(MIC, mic_idx))
        if sys_idx is not None:
            sources.append(DeviceSource(SYSTEM, sys_idx))
    if not sources:
        sys.exit("error: no audio source selected.")
    for s in sources:
        print("audio  " + s.describe(), file=sys.stderr)

    if args.meter:
        run_meter(sources)
        return

    # ---- models (loaded once, warmed up so the first real segment is not slow) --------------------
    from livetr.asr import load_asr, pick_device
    from livetr.output import Printer, Stats
    from livetr.pipeline import Pipeline, Processor
    from livetr.segmenter import SileroVAD
    from livetr.speakers import EcapaEmbedder, OnlineSpeakerClusterer, SpeakerLabeler
    from livetr.translate import NllbTranslator

    t0 = time.monotonic()
    device = pick_device(args.device)
    print(f"loading ASR '{args.asr}' ({MODELS[args.asr].repo}) on {device} …", file=sys.stderr)
    asr = load_asr(args.asr, device)
    print("loading NLLB translator …", file=sys.stderr)
    translator = NllbTranslator(args.nllb_dir)
    embedder = EcapaEmbedder() if any(s.name == SYSTEM for s in sources) else None
    print("warming up …", file=sys.stderr)
    asr.transcribe(np.zeros(16000, dtype=np.float32))
    translator.translate("Բարև")
    if embedder is not None:
        embedder.embed(np.random.default_rng(0).normal(0, 0.01, 16000).astype(np.float32))
    print(f"ready in {time.monotonic() - t0:.0f}s — listening. Ctrl-C to stop.\n", file=sys.stderr)

    clusterer = OnlineSpeakerClusterer(threshold=args.speaker_threshold, max_speakers=args.max_speakers)
    stats = Stats()
    printer = Printer(session_start=time.monotonic(), color=not args.no_color, details=not args.quiet_timings)
    processor = Processor(asr, translator, SpeakerLabeler(embedder, clusterer), printer, stats)
    pipeline = Pipeline(
        sources,
        vad_factory=SileroVAD,
        processor=processor,
        stats=stats,
        segmenter_kwargs=dict(threshold=args.vad_threshold, min_silence_ms=args.min_silence_ms,
                              max_segment_s=args.max_segment_s),
    )
    pipeline.start()
    try:
        pipeline.run_until_stopped()
    finally:
        print(stats.summary(), file=sys.stderr)
"""Terminal output and latency statistics."""
from __future__ import annotations

import sys
from collections import defaultdict
from typing import TextIO

import numpy as np

from livetr.types import Result

LATENCY_BUDGET_S = 5.0

_DIM, _RED, _BOLD, _RESET = "\033[2m", "\033[31m", "\033[1m", "\033[0m"


def fmt_clock(seconds: float) -> str:
    s = max(0, int(seconds))
    return f"{s // 3600:02d}:{s % 3600 // 60:02d}:{s % 60:02d}"


def format_result(result: Result, session_start: float, color: bool = True, details: bool = True) -> str:
    seg = result.segment
    clock = fmt_clock(seg.start_wall - session_start)
    header = f"[{clock}] {result.speaker}"
    if color:
        header = f"{_BOLD}{header}{_RESET}"
    if details:
        t = result.timings
        parts = [f"{result.latency:.1f}s"] + [f"{k} {t[k]:.1f}" for k in ("wait", "asr", "spk", "mt") if k in t]
        if seg.forced_cut:
            parts.append("cut")
        info = "  (" + " · ".join(parts) + ")"
        if color:
            info = (_RED if result.latency > LATENCY_BUDGET_S else _DIM) + info + _RESET
        header += info
    return f"{header}\nHY: {result.hy}\nEN: {result.en}\n"


class Printer:
    def __init__(self, session_start: float, stream: TextIO = sys.stdout, color: bool = True, details: bool = True):
        self.session_start = session_start
        self.stream = stream
        self.color = color
        self.details = details

    def show(self, result: Result) -> None:
        self.stream.write(format_result(result, self.session_start, self.color, self.details) + "\n")
        self.stream.flush()


class Stats:
    """Collects latency per source and per stage; printed at exit."""

    def __init__(self) -> None:
        self.latencies: dict[str, list[float]] = defaultdict(list)
        self.stage_times: dict[str, list[float]] = defaultdict(list)
        self.empty_asr = 0
        self.dropped_short = 0
        self.overflows = 0

    def add(self, result: Result) -> None:
        self.latencies[result.segment.source].append(result.latency)
        for k, v in result.timings.items():
            self.stage_times[k].append(v)

    def summary(self) -> str:
        lines = ["", "── session stats ──"]
        if not self.latencies:
            lines.append("no segments printed")
        for src, vals in sorted(self.latencies.items()):
            a = np.array(vals)
            over = int((a > LATENCY_BUDGET_S).sum())
            lines.append(
                f"{src:<7} n={len(a):<4} latency p50 {np.percentile(a, 50):.2f}s  "
                f"p90 {np.percentile(a, 90):.2f}s  max {a.max():.2f}s  over {LATENCY_BUDGET_S:.0f}s: {over}"
            )
        for k, vals in sorted(self.stage_times.items()):
            lines.append(f"stage {k:<5} mean {np.mean(vals):.2f}s  max {np.max(vals):.2f}s")
        lines.append(
            f"dropped short segments: {self.dropped_short}   empty ASR results: {self.empty_asr}   "
            f"audio overflows: {self.overflows}"
        )
        return "\n".join(lines)
"""Armenian text -> English text with NLLB-200 (CTranslate2, int8, CPU)."""
from __future__ import annotations

from pathlib import Path

DEFAULT_MODEL_DIR = "models/nllb-200-distilled-1.3B-ct2-int8"


class NllbTranslator:
    def __init__(
        self,
        model_dir: str = DEFAULT_MODEL_DIR,
        src_lang: str = "hye_Armn",
        tgt_lang: str = "eng_Latn",
        threads: int = 4,
        beam_size: int = 2,
    ):
        if not (Path(model_dir) / "model.bin").exists():
            raise FileNotFoundError(
                f"No converted NLLB model in '{model_dir}'. Run: python scripts/download_models.py"
            )
        import ctranslate2
        from transformers import AutoTokenizer

        self.tgt_lang = tgt_lang
        self.beam_size = beam_size
        self.tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(model_dir, src_lang=src_lang)
        self.translator = ctranslate2.Translator(
            model_dir, device="cpu", compute_type="int8", intra_threads=threads
        )

    def translate(self, text: str) -> str:
        tokens = self.tokenizer.convert_ids_to_tokens(self.tokenizer.encode(text))
        result = self.translator.translate_batch(
            [tokens],
            target_prefix=[[self.tgt_lang]],
            beam_size=self.beam_size,
            max_decoding_length=256,
        )
        out_tokens = result[0].hypotheses[0][1:]  # drop the target-language token
        return self.tokenizer.decode(
            self.tokenizer.convert_tokens_to_ids(out_tokens), skip_special_tokens=True
        ).strip()
"""Armenian speech -> Armenian text with NVIDIA's Armenian FastConformer (NeMo).

Chosen by measurement on FLEURS (see DECISIONS.md, 2026-10-23). The Whisper and MMS candidates it was compared
against were removed from the code afterwards; their numbers stay in DECISIONS.md and eval/results/.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass

import numpy as np


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class AsrSpec:
    repo: str
    note: str


MODELS: dict[str, AsrSpec] = {
    "nemo-hy": AsrSpec(
        "nvidia/stt_hy_fastconformer_hybrid_large_pc",
        "NVIDIA FastConformer hybrid RNNT/CTC (~115M) trained for Armenian, with punctuation and capitals",
    ),
}


def pick_device(requested: str = "auto") -> str:
    import torch

    if requested != "auto":
        if requested == "mps" and not torch.backends.mps.is_available():
            raise RuntimeError("--device mps requested but MPS is not available on this machine.")
        return requested
    return "mps" if torch.backends.mps.is_available() else "cpu"


class NemoASR:
    def __init__(self, repo: str, device: str):
        from pathlib import Path

        from huggingface_hub import snapshot_download
        from nemo.collections.asr.models import ASRModel

        files = sorted(Path(snapshot_download(repo, allow_patterns=["*.nemo"])).glob("*.nemo"))
        if len(files) != 1:
            raise RuntimeError(f"expected one .nemo file in {repo}, found {[f.name for f in files]}")
        self.model = ASRModel.restore_from(str(files[0]), map_location=device).eval()

    def transcribe(self, audio: np.ndarray) -> str:
        out = self.model.transcribe([audio], batch_size=1, verbose=False)
        return out[0].text.strip()


def load_asr(key: str, device: str) -> NemoASR:
    if key not in MODELS:
        raise ValueError(f"Unknown ASR model '{key}'. Choose from: {', '.join(MODELS)}")
    return NemoASR(MODELS[key].repo, device)
"""Data passed between pipeline stages."""
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass, field

import numpy as np

MIC = "mic"
SYSTEM = "system"


@dataclass
class Segment:
    """One utterance cut out by the VAD."""

    source: str          # MIC or SYSTEM
    audio: np.ndarray    # float32, mono, 16 kHz
    start_wall: float    # time.monotonic() of the first sample
    end_wall: float      # time.monotonic() of the last speech sample = "end of phrase"
    forced_cut: bool = False  # True if cut at max length instead of at a pause

    @property
    def duration(self) -> float:
        from livetr import SAMPLE_RATE
        return len(self.audio) / SAMPLE_RATE


@dataclass
class Result:
    """What gets printed for one segment."""

    segment: Segment
    speaker: str
    hy: str
    en: str
    printed_wall: float
    timings: dict[str, float] = field(default_factory=dict)  # seconds per stage

    @property
    def latency(self) -> float:
        """End of phrase -> line printed."""
        return self.printed_wall - self.segment.end_wall
"""R5/C1: utterances are cut at pauses, long speech is force-cut, noise blips are dropped."""
import numpy as np
import pytest

from conftest import SR, energy_vad, silence, tone
from livetr.segmenter import Segmenter


def feed(seg: Segmenter, audio: np.ndarray, block: int = 512, t0: float = 100.0):
    """Push audio in blocks with consistent wall-clock timestamps, return all segments (incl. flush)."""
    out = []
    for start in range(0, len(audio), block):
        chunk = audio[start:start + block]
        out += seg.push(chunk, t0 + (start + len(chunk)) / SR)
    return out + seg.flush()


def test_one_phrase_between_silences():
    audio = np.concatenate([silence(1.0), tone(2.0), silence(1.0)])
    segs = feed(Segmenter("mic", energy_vad), audio)
    assert len(segs) == 1
    s = segs[0]
    assert s.source == "mic" and not s.forced_cut
    assert 2.0 <= s.duration <= 2.0 + 2 * 0.16 + 0.07  # speech + padding on both sides (frame rounding)
    # end of phrase = end of the tone at t0 + 3.0 s, within one frame
    assert s.end_wall == pytest.approx(100.0 + 3.0, abs=0.04)
    assert s.start_wall == pytest.approx(100.0 + 1.0 - 0.16, abs=0.05)


def test_two_phrases_with_short_pause_merge_long_pause_splits():
    short_pause = np.concatenate([tone(1.0), silence(0.3), tone(1.0), silence(1.0)])
    assert len(feed(Segmenter("mic", energy_vad, min_silence_ms=500), short_pause)) == 1
    long_pause = np.concatenate([tone(1.0), silence(0.8), tone(1.0), silence(1.0)])
    assert len(feed(Segmenter("mic", energy_vad, min_silence_ms=500), long_pause)) == 2


def test_short_blip_is_dropped_and_counted():
    seg = Segmenter("system", energy_vad, min_speech_ms=400)
    segs = feed(seg, np.concatenate([silence(0.5), tone(0.2), silence(1.0)]))
    assert segs == []
    assert seg.dropped_short == 1


def test_long_speech_is_force_cut():
    seg = Segmenter("system", energy_vad, max_segment_s=8.0)
    segs = feed(seg, np.concatenate([tone(20.0), silence(1.0)]))
    assert [s.forced_cut for s in segs] == [True, True, False]
    assert all(s.duration <= 8.0 + 0.2 for s in segs)
    assert sum(s.duration for s in segs) == pytest.approx(20.0, abs=0.5)


@pytest.mark.parametrize("block", [160, 512, 1000, 4800])
def test_block_size_does_not_change_result(block):
    audio = np.concatenate([silence(0.7), tone(1.5), silence(0.9), tone(1.2), silence(1.0)])
    ref = feed(Segmenter("mic", energy_vad), audio, block=512)
    got = feed(Segmenter("mic", energy_vad), audio, block=block)
    assert len(got) == len(ref) == 2
    for a, b in zip(ref, got):
        assert np.array_equal(a.audio, b.audio)
        assert a.end_wall == pytest.approx(b.end_wall, abs=1e-6)


def test_flush_emits_pending_speech():
    seg = Segmenter("mic", energy_vad)
    out = []
    audio = np.concatenate([silence(0.3), tone(1.0)])
    for start in range(0, len(audio), 512):
        out += seg.push(audio[start:start + 512], 1.0)
    assert out == []
    assert len(seg.flush()) == 1


def test_rejects_wrong_dtype():
    with pytest.raises(ValueError):
        Segmenter("mic", energy_vad).push(np.zeros(512, dtype=np.float64), 0.0)
import sys
from pathlib import Path

import numpy as np
import pytest

sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent))

SR = 16000


def energy_vad(frame: np.ndarray) -> float:
    """Stand-in for Silero: 'speech' = loud frame. Lets us test segmentation logic without a model."""
    return 1.0 if float(np.abs(frame).mean()) > 0.01 else 0.0


def tone(seconds: float, amp: float = 0.3, freq: float = 220.0) -> np.ndarray:
    t = np.arange(int(seconds * SR)) / SR
    return (amp * np.sin(2 * np.pi * freq * t)).astype(np.float32)


def silence(seconds: float) -> np.ndarray:
    return np.zeros(int(seconds * SR), dtype=np.float32)


@pytest.fixture
def vad():
    return energy_vad
# Tests and QA checklist

Derived from the brief's requirements, not from the code. Tick items by hand before the interview.
Automated tests: `pytest` (pure logic only, no models or audio devices needed).

## R1 — Two audio sources at once
- [x] `python -m livetr --list-devices` shows the mic and `BlackHole 2ch`. (2026-10-23)
- [x] `python -m livetr --meter` shows level bars moving for mic when I talk and for system when a video plays.
  System: OK (−14 dB). Mic: −90 dB at first (no microphone permission); after granting it −52 dB (2026-10-23).
- [x] I still hear the call while BlackHole captures it (Multi-Output Device selected as output).
- [x] Wrong device name → program stops with a clear error listing the input devices. (automated: `test_devices.py`)
- [x] Ambiguous device name (matches 2 devices) → clear error. (automated)

  214 h); card reports FLEURS test WER 12.3 %, consistent with our 10.0 % on shorter clips. Licence CC-BY-4.0.
- Caveat: FLEURS is in its training data (like buzz), so FLEURS favours both; live tests decide for real speech.
  The large non-FLEURS share (incl. spontaneous-ish proprietary data) is a reason to expect it to hold up better.
- Runs on CPU by default: as fast as MPS on this model, no MPS warm-up spike, and the GPU stays free.

  1.1–1.8 s after they end. Spikes (translation up to 10 s) were traced to the machine, not the code: a fixed numpy
  workload varied 5.8x in the same minutes, while another project was rendering speech (`say`) in the background,
  on battery, with ~4 GB swap. Latency must be measured on a quiet Mac (other jobs stopped, on power).

## 2026-10-23 — Speaker threshold 0.45 confirmed on FLEURS pairs (`eval/fleurs_speakers.py`)
ECAPA cosine similarity for all 832 pairs of FLEURS test recordings of the same sentence (932 clips).
Different-gender pairs (certainly different people, n=401): max 0.30 on full clips, 0.27 on 3 s crops.
Same-gender pairs (n=431) are bimodal: different people ~0.1–0.4, the same reader twice ~0.6–1.0 (full clips)
or ~0.5–0.9 (3 s crops). The valley is 0.4–0.5 in both cases, so 0.45 stays: no different-gender pair reaches it,
and it sits below the same-reader peak even for short phrases.
Limits: read speech, studio-like audio, no call codec. Two similar same-gender voices can reach 0.4–0.5 and may be
merged; that is the known hard case and needs a live check. Pair similarities are in `eval/results/`.

## 2026-10-23 — End-to-end test call with known speakers (`eval/make_test_call.py`, `eval/score_call.py`)
FLEURS Armenian has only **three voices**: one man and two women (every male test clip matches every other male
clip at >= 0.6; women form two groups). So the ASR test set also had only 3 speakers, and a 4-person test call is
not possible from FLEURS. A first version of the test call took "two men" who were the same man reading two
sentences (similarity 0.74); the program merged them, correctly. The builder now accepts a reader only if all
their clips are < 0.35 against the other readers.
Test call: 3 speakers (2 women, 1 man), 9 turns, 9 different sentences, 144 s; every speaker comes back twice.
| run | speaker turns right | speakers found | CER / WER | latency p50 / max |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `--file`, clean | 9/9 | 3 of 3 (+1 `Speaker ?`) | 5.7 % / 13.0 % | 1.2 / 1.5 s |
| `--file`, through Opus 16 kbps | 9/9 | 3 of 3 (+1 `Speaker ?`) | 5.4 % / 12.2 % | 1.2 / 1.5 s |
| **live**: played through Multi-Output → BlackHole | 9/9 | 3 of 3 (+1 `Speaker ?`) | 5.8 % / 12.6 % | **1.3 / 1.6 s** |
Reading:
- Latency: ~1.3 s, far under 5 s; translation (0.7 s) is now the slowest stage, ASR 0.2 s.
- The call codec barely matters for ASR or speakers.
- CER 5.7 % vs 3.3 % on single clips: turns here are 10–29 s, so the VAD force-cuts at 8 s, sometimes inside a
  word, and both halves are transcribed and translated separately. Possible fix: cut at the quietest moment of
  the last ~1 s instead of exactly at 8 s.
- `Speaker ?` appeared once, on a short tail after a cut (< 1 s): by design (short segments don't get a guess).
- Not covered: two *very* similar voices (FLEURS has none), spontaneous speech, the mic side.

## 2026-10-23 — Local only, verified
The program ran end to end inside a macOS sandbox that blocks all outgoing network connections
(`sandbox-exec ... (deny network-outbound ...)`; a test request to huggingface.co failed inside it).

## 2026-10-23 — Microphone side live
Author speaking colloquial Armenian into the built-in mic (after granting microphone permission; before that
macOS delivered digital silence, −90 dB): 6 phrases, all `Me`, latency 0.8–1.5 s. ASR handled colloquial forms
well; NLLB mistranslated idioms and loanwords (`ինչ կա, չկա` → "there is no such thing", `չատերը` → "chessboard").
Translation of spoken, colloquial Armenian is now the weakest part. Options for later: NLLB 1.3B (memory),
or a small local LLM for translation (memory and latency); not tried within the time budget.

## 2026-10-23 — Phrase limit (forced cut) 8 s -> 15 s
The 8 s limit was set when ASR took 2–3 s. With the NVIDIA model (0.2–0.3 s) it only cost accuracy: cuts fell
inside words (`քարանձավ` -> "…մի" + "Անցավ…"). Test call (clean, 9 turns of 7–29 s), one run each:
| limit | forced cuts | CER / WER | latency p50 / max | speaker turns |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 8 s | 11 | 5.7 % / 13.0 % | 1.2 / 1.5 s | 9/9 |
| 12 s | 6 | 5.0 % / 11.4 % | 1.9 / 3.0 s | 9/9 |
| 15 s | 3 | 4.1 % / 10.2 % | 2.1 / 2.8 s | 9/9 |
Chosen: 15 s. Trade-off: in a long monologue the first words appear later (up to ~15 s after they were said); the
brief measures latency from the end of a phrase, which stays < 3 s. Not done: cutting at the quietest moment.

## 2026-10-23 — Translation: NLLB 1.3B instead of 600M (600M stays as fallback)
`eval/run_mt_fleurs.py`: 150 FLEURS test sentences, Armenian -> English, chrF against the official English FLEURS
translations; plus the 6 colloquial phrases from the live mic test (`eval/colloquial.tsv`, judged by eye).
| model | chrF | MT time mean / p90 / max | memory (int8) |
|---|---|---|---|
| NLLB 600M | 60.1 | 1.00 / 1.43 / 2.59 s | 0.6 GB |
| NLLB 1.3B | 63.5 | 1.81 / 2.53 / 4.68 s | 1.4 GB |
Colloquial: 1.3B fixes the worst errors (`ինչպես ես ինչ կա, չկա` -> "Hi, how are you?" instead of "Hello, as I am,
there is no such thing"; `չատերը` -> "chat" instead of "chessboard").
Whole program with 1.3B, test call, 15 s phrase limit, on battery: latency p50 3.0 s, p90 3.6 s, max 4.0 s, 0 over
5 s; speakers 9/9; peak memory 3.6 GB (2.8 GB with 600M).
Chosen: 1.3B, because translation was the weakest stage (tip 2: improve the weakest link). Trade-off: the latency
margin shrinks from ~2 s to ~1 s. If the practice call (with a call app using CPU) goes over 5 s, the default goes
back to 600M. Conversion needs `load_as_float16=True, low_cpu_mem_usage=True` to fit in 8 GB RAM.

```
"""Build a test "call" from FLEURS with known speakers, to replay through the whole program.

    python eval/make_test_call.py
    python -m livetr --file eval/data/test_call.wav --no-mic --no-color | tee eval/data/test_call_out.txt
    python eval/score_call.py eval/data/test_call_out.txt

FLEURS has no speaker ids, and its Armenian test set has only three voices: one man and two women (every male
clip matches every other male clip at >= 0.6). A reader is a clip plus its closest clips of other sentences,
all with ECAPA similarity >= --min-sim (0.7); different people never come close to that (man/woman pairs
max 0.30, see fleurs_speakers.py). A reader is only accepted if all its clips are below --max-cross (0.35)
against the readers already chosen, so the ground truth is reliable. A first version took "two men" that were
the same man reading two sentences (similarity 0.74); this check prevents that.
Three speakers, three turns each, in an order where every speaker comes back after others have spoken.
Limit: no two *very* similar voices, because FLEURS does not have them.

Writes eval/data/test_call.wav, test_call_opus16k.wav (through the 16 kbps Opus codec that calls use, needs
ffmpeg) and test_call.tsv (start, end, speaker, reference text).
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import csv
import io
import subprocess
import tarfile
import wave
from pathlib import Path

import numpy as np

from common import DATA, ROOT

GAP_S = 1.2
ORDER = ["F1", "M1", "F2", "M1", "F1", "F2", "M1", "F2", "F1"]   # every speaker comes back after others
TURNS = 3


def save(path: Path, audio: np.ndarray) -> None:
    with wave.open(str(path), "wb") as w:
        w.setnchannels(1)
        w.setsampwidth(2)
        w.setframerate(16000)
        w.writeframes((np.clip(audio, -1, 1) * 32767).astype(np.int16).tobytes())


def main() -> None:
    p = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    p.add_argument("--min-sim", type=float, default=0.7)
    p.add_argument("--max-cross", type=float, default=0.35)
    args = p.parse_args()

    import soundfile as sf
    from huggingface_hub import hf_hub_download

    import sys
    sys.path.insert(0, str(ROOT))
    from livetr.speakers import EcapaEmbedder

    tsv = hf_hub_download("google/fleurs", "data/hy_am/test.tsv", repo_type="dataset")
    with open(tsv, encoding="utf-8") as f:
        meta = {r[1]: dict(sentence=r[0], text=r[2], gender=r[6]) for r in csv.reader(f, delimiter="\t", quoting=csv.QUOTE_NONE)}
    archive = hf_hub_download("google/fleurs", "data/hy_am/audio/test.tar.gz", repo_type="dataset")

    def read_tar(names: set[str] | None):
        with tarfile.open(archive, "r:gz") as tar:
            for m in tar:
                name = Path(m.name).name
                if m.isfile() and name in meta and (names is None or name in names):
                    a, sr = sf.read(io.BytesIO(tar.extractfile(m).read()), dtype="float32")
                    if sr != 16000 or a.ndim != 1:
                        raise ValueError(f"{name}: expected 16 kHz mono")
                    yield name, a

    embedder = EcapaEmbedder()
    names, vecs = [], []
    for name, a in read_tar(None):  # embed every clip, keep only the 192-d vectors in memory
        e = embedder.embed(a)
        names.append(name)
        vecs.append(e / np.linalg.norm(e))
    V = np.array(vecs)
    S = V @ V.T

    # FLEURS Armenian has only three voices (one man, two women: every male clip matches every other at >= 0.6).
    # A reader = a seed clip plus its closest clips of other sentences (all >= min_sim). A new reader is accepted
    # only if all its clips are below max_cross against every clip already taken.
    chosen: dict[str, list[list[int]]] = {"FEMALE": [], "MALE": []}
    need = {"FEMALE": 2, "MALE": 1}
    taken: list[int] = []
    for i, name in enumerate(names):
        g = meta[name]["gender"]
        if len(chosen[g]) >= need[g] or any(S[i, k] >= args.max_cross for k in taken):
            continue
        members, sentences = [], set()
        for j in np.argsort(-S[i]):
            if meta[names[j]]["gender"] == g and meta[names[j]]["sentence"] not in sentences and S[i, j] >= args.min_sim:
                members.append(int(j))
                sentences.add(meta[names[j]]["sentence"])
            if len(members) == TURNS:
                break
        if len(members) == TURNS and all(S[m, k] < args.max_cross for m in members for k in taken):
            chosen[g].append(members)
            taken += members
    if any(len(chosen[g]) < need[g] for g in need):
        raise SystemExit(f"not enough distinct readers: {[(k, len(v)) for k, v in chosen.items()]}")
    speakers = {"F1": chosen["FEMALE"][0], "F2": chosen["FEMALE"][1], "M1": chosen["MALE"][0]}
    cross = max(S[a, b] for sa, ma in speakers.items() for sb, mb in speakers.items() if sa != sb
                for a in ma for b in mb)
    same = {spk: min(S[a, b] for a in m for b in m if a != b) for spk, m in speakers.items()}
    taken_pairs = [(spk, k) for spk, m in speakers.items() for k in m]
    audio = dict(read_tar({names[k] for _, k in taken_pairs}))
    text = {n: meta[n]["text"] for n in audio}

    turn_of = {k: 0 for k in speakers}
    parts, rows, t = [np.zeros(int(GAP_S * 16000), np.float32)], [], GAP_S
    for spk in ORDER:
        name = names[speakers[spk][turn_of[spk]]]
        turn_of[spk] += 1
        a = audio[name]
        rows.append((f"{t:.2f}", f"{t + len(a) / 16000:.2f}", spk, text[name]))
        parts += [a, np.zeros(int(GAP_S * 16000), np.float32)]
        t += len(a) / 16000 + GAP_S
    call = np.concatenate(parts)
    call *= 0.5 / max(np.abs(call).max(), 1e-6)  # same level for both versions

    out = DATA / "test_call.wav"
    save(out, call)
    with open(DATA / "test_call.tsv", "w", encoding="utf-8", newline="") as f:
        w = csv.writer(f, delimiter="\t")
        w.writerow(["start", "end", "speaker", "reference"])
        w.writerows(rows)
    opus = DATA / "test_call_opus16k.wav"
    subprocess.run(f"ffmpeg -y -loglevel error -i {out} -c:a libopus -b:a 16k -f ogg - | "
                   f"ffmpeg -y -loglevel error -i - -ar 16000 -ac 1 -c:a pcm_s16le {opus}", shell=True, check=True)
    print(f"{len(call) / 16000:.0f} s, {len(rows)} turns, lowest similarity within a reader "
          + ", ".join(f"{k} {v:.2f}" for k, v in same.items()))
    print(f"largest similarity between two different chosen readers: {cross:.2f}")
    print(f"wrote {out}, {opus}, {DATA / 'test_call.tsv'}")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
"""Score the program's output on the test call: speaker labels, transcription errors, latency.

    python eval/score_call.py eval/data/test_call_out.txt [--offset 0]

Each printed phrase is matched to the true turn that contains its start time ([hh:mm:ss] in the header, ±1 s).
Speaker labels are scored per turn: a turn is right if its label is the label its speaker got on its first
turn, and different speakers never share a label. --offset shifts the printed times (live playback starts a
little after the program, so its clock runs ahead).
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import csv
import re
import sys
from collections import Counter

from common import DATA, ROOT

sys.path.insert(0, str(ROOT))
from livetr.metrics import cer_counts, wer_counts  # noqa: E402

HEADER = re.compile(r"^\[(\d\d):(\d\d):(\d\d)\] (Me|Speaker [0-9?]+)\s+\(([\d.]+)s")


def main() -> None:
    p = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    p.add_argument("output")
    p.add_argument("--offset", type=float, default=0.0)
    args = p.parse_args()

    with open(DATA / "test_call.tsv", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        turns = [dict(r, start=float(r["start"]), end=float(r["end"])) for r in csv.DictReader(f, delimiter="\t")]
    phrases, cur = [], None
    for line in open(args.output, encoding="utf-8"):
        m = HEADER.match(line)
        if m:
            h, mi, s, label, lat = m.groups()
            cur = dict(t=int(h) * 3600 + int(mi) * 60 + int(s) - args.offset, label=label, latency=float(lat), hy="")
            phrases.append(cur)
        elif cur is not None and line.startswith("HY:"):
            cur["hy"] = line[3:].strip()

    for ph in phrases:
        ph["turn"] = next((i for i, t in enumerate(turns) if t["start"] - 1.5 <= ph["t"] <= t["end"]), None)
    unmatched = [ph for ph in phrases if ph["turn"] is None]

    first_label: dict[str, str] = {}
    right = 0
    print(f"{'turn':>4} {'true':>4}  labels printed")
    for i, t in enumerate(turns):
        labels = [ph["label"] for ph in phrases if ph["turn"] == i]
        main_label = Counter(labels).most_common(1)[0][0] if labels else "-"
        first_label.setdefault(t["speaker"], main_label)
        ok = main_label == first_label[t["speaker"]] and labels != []
        right += ok
        print(f"{i + 1:>4} {t['speaker']:>4}  {', '.join(labels) or '(nothing)'}  {'ok' if ok else 'WRONG'}")
    shared = len(set(first_label.values())) < len(first_label)

    c_err = c_tot = w_err = w_tot = 0
    for i, t in enumerate(turns):
        hyp = " ".join(ph["hy"] for ph in phrases if ph["turn"] == i)
        ce, ct = cer_counts(t["reference"], hyp)
        we, wt = wer_counts(t["reference"], hyp)
        c_err, c_tot, w_err, w_tot = c_err + ce, c_tot + ct, w_err + we, w_tot + wt
    lats = sorted(ph["latency"] for ph in phrases)

    found = {ph["label"] for ph in phrases if ph["label"] != "Speaker ?"}
    unknown = sum(ph["label"] == "Speaker ?" for ph in phrases)
    print(f"\nspeaker turns right: {right}/{len(turns)}; speakers found: {len(found)} (true {len(first_label)}); "
          f"'Speaker ?' phrases: {unknown}{'; TWO SPEAKERS SHARE A LABEL' if shared else ''}")
    print(f"transcription: CER {c_err / c_tot:.1%}  WER {w_err / w_tot:.1%}")
    print(f"latency: n={len(lats)}  p50 {lats[len(lats) // 2]:.2f}s  max {lats[-1]:.2f}s  over 5 s: "
          f"{sum(x > 5 for x in lats)}")
    if unmatched:
        print(f"{len(unmatched)} phrase(s) outside any turn: {[round(ph['t'], 1) for ph in unmatched]}")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

        )

    def _callback(self, indata, frames, time_info, status) -> None:
        # Runs on the PortAudio thread: only copy and enqueue, no heavy work here.
        if status.input_overflow:
            self.overflows += 1
        self.blocks.put(RawBlock(indata.copy(), time.monotonic()))

    def start(self) -> None:
        self._stream.start()

    def stop(self) -> None:
        self._stream.stop()
        self._stream.close()

    def describe(self) -> str:
        return f"{self.name}: [{self.device_index}] {self.device_name} ({self.channels} ch, {self.sample_rate} Hz)"


class FileSource:
    """Plays a 16-bit PCM WAV file into the pipeline at real-time speed, as if it came from a device.

    Used for the walking skeleton, for latency tests and for rehearsals without a call.
    """

    def __init__(self, name: str, path: str, blocksize_ms: int = 32, realtime: bool = True):
        with wave.open(path, "rb") as w:
            if w.getsampwidth() != 2:
                raise ValueError(
                    f"{path}: only 16-bit PCM WAV is supported. Convert with: "
                    f"afconvert -f WAVE -d LEI16 in.m4a out.wav"
                )
            self.sample_rate = w.getframerate()
            self.channels = w.getnchannels()
            pcm = np.frombuffer(w.readframes(w.getnframes()), dtype=np.int16)
        self.name = name
        self.path = path
        self.audio = (pcm.reshape(-1, self.channels).astype(np.float32) / 32768.0)
        self.blocksize = int(self.sample_rate * blocksize_ms / 1000)
        self.realtime = realtime
        self.blocks: queue.Queue[RawBlock] = queue.Queue()
        self.overflows = 0
        self.done = threading.Event()
        self._thread = threading.Thread(target=self._run, name=f"file-{name}", daemon=True)
        self._stop = threading.Event()

    def _run(self) -> None:
        t0 = time.monotonic()
        for start in range(0, len(self.audio), self.blocksize):
            if self._stop.is_set():
                break
            block = self.audio[start:start + self.blocksize]
            if self.realtime:
                due = t0 + (start + len(block)) / self.sample_rate
                delay = due - time.monotonic()
                if delay > 0:
                    time.sleep(delay)
            self.blocks.put(RawBlock(block, time.monotonic()))
        # One second of silence so the VAD sees the final pause.
        tail = np.zeros((self.sample_rate, self.channels), dtype=np.float32)
        if self.realtime:
            time.sleep(1.0)
        self.blocks.put(RawBlock(tail, time.monotonic()))
        self.done.set()

    def start(self) -> None:
        self._thread.start()

    def stop(self) -> None:
        self._stop.set()

    def describe(self) -> str:
        return f"{self.name}: file {self.path} ({self.channels} ch, {self.sample_rate} Hz)"
"""Helpers shared by the evaluation scripts."""
from __future__ import annotations

import csv
import resource
import sys
import wave
from pathlib import Path

import numpy as np

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
DATA = ROOT / "eval" / "data"
RESULTS = ROOT / "eval" / "results"


def load_manifest(path: Path = DATA / "manifest.tsv") -> list[dict]:
    if not path.exists():
        raise SystemExit(f"{path} not found. Create it first: eval/record.py (own clips) or eval/get_fleurs.py (FLEURS)")
    with open(path, encoding="utf-8") as f:
        return list(csv.DictReader(f, delimiter="\t"))


def load_wav(path: Path) -> np.ndarray:
    with wave.open(str(path), "rb") as w:
        if w.getframerate() != 16000 or w.getnchannels() != 1 or w.getsampwidth() != 2:
            raise ValueError(f"{path}: expected 16 kHz mono 16-bit WAV")
        pcm = np.frombuffer(w.readframes(w.getnframes()), dtype=np.int16)
    return pcm.astype(np.float32) / 32768.0


def peak_rss_gb() -> float:
    rss = resource.getrusage(resource.RUSAGE_SELF).ru_maxrss
    return rss / 1e9 if sys.platform == "darwin" else rss * 1024 / 1e9  # bytes on macOS, KiB on Linux

wave\n\nimport numpy as np\n\nfrom common import DATA\n\nSR = 16000\nSECTION_S = 60\nrng = np.random.default_rng(0)\n\n\ndef db(x: np.ndarray, level_db: float) -> np.ndarray:\n    rms = float(np.sqrt(np.mean(x ** 2))) or 1.0\n    return x * (10 ** (level_db / 20) / rms)\n\n\ndef coloured(n: int, exponent: float) -> np.ndarray:\n    \"\"\"Noise with power ~ 1/f^exponent (1 = pink, 2 =     python eval/record.py --speaker anna                 # reads eval/sentences.tsv
    python eval/record.py --speaker anna --mic 2

import threading
import wave

```
"""Build an ASR test set from Google FLEURS Armenian (hy_am): read speech with reference text, many readers.

    python eval/get_fleurs.py                      # 40 clips <= 10 s from the test split
    python eval/get_fleurs.py --n 50 --max-seconds 8

Output: eval/data/fleurs/<file>.wav (16 kHz mono 16-bit) and eval/data/fleurs_manifest.tsv (path, speaker,
reference). It is a separate manifest on purpose: FLEURS has no speaker ids, so these clips must not go into
eval/data/manifest.tsv, which run_speakers.py reads. Benchmark with: python eval/run_asr.py --set fleurs --model ...

The test split is used because the ASR fine-tunes may have trained on FLEURS train. The archive (~520 MB) is
cached by huggingface_hub; only the chosen clips are written to eval/data/ (git-ignored).
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import csv
import io
import random
import tarfile
import wave
from pathlib import Path

import numpy as np

from common import DATA

REPO = "google/fleurs"
LANG = "hy_am"
OUT_DIR = DATA / "fleurs"
MANIFEST = DATA / "fleurs_manifest.tsv"


def pick(rows: list[list[str]], n: int, max_seconds: float, seed: int) -> list[list[str]]:
    """One recording per sentence (FLEURS has several readers per sentence), length <= max_seconds."""
    by_sentence: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
    for r in rows:
        if int(r[5]) / 16000 <= max_seconds:
            by_sentence.setdefault(r[0], r)
    if len(by_sentence) < n:
        raise SystemExit(f"only {len(by_sentence)} sentences <= {max_seconds}s, asked for {n}")
    return random.Random(seed).sample(sorted(by_sentence.values()), n)


def read_wav_16k_mono(data: bytes, name: str) -> np.ndarray:
    import soundfile as sf

    audio, sr = sf.read(io.BytesIO(data), dtype="float32")
    if sr != 16000 or audio.ndim != 1:
        raise ValueError(f"{name}: expected 16 kHz mono, got {sr} Hz, shape {audio.shape}")
    return audio


def save_wav(path: Path, audio: np.ndarray) -> None:
    pcm = (np.clip(audio, -1, 1) * 32767).astype(np.int16)
    with wave.open(str(path), "wb") as w:
        w.setnchannels(1)
        w.setsampwidth(2)
        w.setframerate(16000)
        w.writeframes(pcm.tobytes())


def main() -> None:
    p = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    p.add_argument("--n", type=int, default=40, help="number of clips")
    p.add_argument("--max-seconds", type=float, default=10.0, help="skip longer clips (live phrases are cut at 8 s)")
    p.add_argument("--split", default="test", choices=["test", "dev"])
    p.add_argument("--seed", type=int, default=0)
    args = p.parse_args()

    from huggingface_hub import hf_hub_download

    tsv = hf_hub_download(REPO, f"data/{LANG}/{args.split}.tsv", repo_type="dataset")
    with open(tsv, encoding="utf-8") as f:
        # columns: id, file name, raw transcription, normalised transcription, phonemes, samples, gender
        rows = list(csv.reader(f, delimiter="\t", quoting=csv.QUOTE_NONE))
    chosen = pick(rows, args.n, args.max_seconds, args.seed)
    wanted = {r[1]: r for r in chosen}

    print(f"downloading {args.split} audio archive (cached after the first run)…")
    archive = hf_hub_download(REPO, f"data/{LANG}/audio/{args.split}.tar.gz", repo_type="dataset")

    OUT_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    found: dict[str, np.ndarray] = {}
    with tarfile.open(archive, "r:gz") as tar:
        for member in tar:
            name = Path(member.name).name
            if member.isfile() and name in wanted:
                found[name] = read_wav_16k_mono(tar.extractfile(member).read(), name)
    missing = set(wanted) - set(found)
    if missing:
        raise SystemExit(f"{len(missing)} chosen files not in the archive, e.g. {sorted(missing)[:3]}")

    total = 0.0
    with open(MANIFEST, "w", encoding="utf-8", newline="") as mf:
        w = csv.writer(mf, delimiter="\t")
        w.writerow(["path", "speaker", "reference"])
        for r in chosen:
            rel = Path("fleurs") / r[1]
            save_wav(DATA / rel, found[r[1]])
            w.writerow([str(rel), f"fleurs-{r[6].lower()}", r[2]])
            total += len(found[r[1]]) / 16000
    print(f"wrote {len(chosen)} clips ({total:.0f} s) to {OUT_DIR} and {MANIFEST}")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
"""Benchmark one ASR model on the recorded clips: CER, WER, latency, memory.

    python eval/run_asr.py --model nemo-hy                 # own recordings (eval/record.py)
    python eval/run_asr.py --set fleurs --model nemo-hy    # FLEURS clips from eval/get_fleurs.py
    python eval/run_asr.py --summary

One model per process so peak memory is measured cleanly. Each run appends one row to
eval/results/asr_results.csv and writes per-clip output to eval/results/asr_<model>.tsv
(asr_<model>_fleurs.tsv for the FLEURS set).
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import csv
import os
import sys
import time

import numpy as np

from common import DATA, RESULTS, ROOT, load_manifest, load_wav, peak_rss_gb

sys.path.insert(0, str(ROOT))
from livetr.asr import MODELS, load_asr, pick_device  # noqa: E402
from livetr.metrics import cer_counts, wer_counts  # noqa: E402

SUMMARY = RESULTS / "asr_results.csv"
SETS = {"own": DATA / "manifest.tsv", "fleurs": DATA / "fleurs_manifest.tsv"}
FIELDS = ["set", "model", "device", "dtype", "clips", "cer", "wer", "lat_mean", "lat_p90", "lat_max", "rtf", "peak_rss_gb",
          "load_s", "date"]


def run(model: str, device: str, clip_set: str) -> None:
    os.environ.setdefault("HF_HUB_OFFLINE", "1")
    rows = [r for r in load_manifest(SETS[clip_set]) if r["reference"].strip()]
    if not rows:
        raise SystemExit("no clips with a reference transcript in the manifest")

    t0 = time.monotonic()
    asr = load_asr(model, pick_device(device))
```

### Assistant · 2026-10-24 18:26:17

**Tool call: `Bash`**

```json
{
  "command": "cat >> DECISIONS.md <<'EOF'\n\n## 2026-10-24 — Forced cut at the quietest moment of the last second\nA phrase longer than the limit used to be cut exactly at the limit, often inside a word. Now the segmenter looks at\nthe last 1 s (`cut_search_ms=1000`) and cuts after the quietest 32 ms frame (lowest RMS), if it is a clear dip\n(below half the window's median RMS); without a clear dip it still cuts at the limit. The rest of the audio starts\nthe next segment, so nothing is lost or doubled (unit tests). Test call (`--file`, 9 turns), one run each:\n| limit | cut | forced cuts | CER / WER | speaker turns |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| 8 s | at the limit (old) | 11 | 5.7 % / 13.0 % | 9/9 |\n| 8 s | quietest moment | 11 | 5.1 % / 11.8 % | 9/9 |\n| 15 s | at the limit (old) | 3 | 4.1 % / 10.2 % | 9/9 |\n| 15 s | quietest moment | 3 | 4.0 % / 9.8 % | 9/9 |\nCost: a cut line now ends up to 1 s before the moment the cut is made, and its latency is measured from that\nearlier point (honest: the line holds audio only up to there), so cut lines show up to +1 s in `wait`.\nLatency of these four runs is not comparable: the Mac had 7 GB swap in use and translation took 2-4 s instead of\n1-2 s (a 4.4 GB Ollama `llama-server` from another project started during the runs). Re-measure on a quiet Mac.\nEOF\n.venv/bin/python - <<'EOF'\nfrom pathlib import Path\np = Path(\"TESTS.md\"); s = p.read_text()\na = \"- [x] Long monologue (> 15 s) is force-cut and each part arrives in time. (automated + test call; cuts can split words)\"\nassert a in s\ns = s.replace(a, \"- [x] Long monologue (> 15 s) is force-cut and each part arrives in time. (automated + test call)\\n\"\n \"- [x] Forced cut goes into a gap between words when there is one in the last 1 s (automated: `test_segmenter.py`;\\n\"\n \"  test call at 8 s limit: CER 5.7 % -> 5.1 %).\")\np.write_text(s)\nEOF\n.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q 2>&1 | tail -1 && git add livetr/segmenter.py tests/test_segmenter.py DECISIONS.md TESTS.md && git commit -q -m \"Segmenter: forced cut at the quietest moment of the last second (test call CER 5.7 -> 5.1 % at 8 s)\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>\" && git log --oneline -1",
  "description": "Document cut decision, run tests, commit"
}
```

I've done what can be done without a quiet Mac or a live call. The timing tests are blocked: Ollama is still running with a model loaded. Tell me when your lawyer app and Ollama are stopped, and I'll run the timing tests (about 25 minutes, script ready). They cover both sides at once, start-up time, a 10-minute session with swap watching, and numbers and questions.

**Done:**
- **Cut long turns at the quietest moment.** A cut longer than the limit now goes into a gap between words in the last second. On the test call, CER went from 5.7% to 5.1% with an 8 s limit, and from 4.1% to 4.0% with the default 15 s. The cost: a cut line can show up to 1 s more in its `wait` time. Tests pass; committed as e448fb0.
- **Silence and noise, synthetic half.** 5 minutes of room tone, fan, typing, thumps and breaths printed 0 lines; the voice detector never triggered.
- **Test tools** (committed as 733df3a): `--mic-file`, so two files can play as both sides at once; a 10+ minute test call with 36 turns and no speaker twice in a row; and a numbers/questions check on FLEURS.

**Waiting for the quiet Mac:**
- **Both sides talking at once:** checks the queue wait and that latency stays under 5 s.
- **Warm-up and start-up time:** seconds from command to listening, and whether the first phrase is slow.
- **10 minutes without swap.** This is a recording, not Zoom. A real call app will use extra memory, so please also watch Activity Monitor during the rehearsal call.
- **Labels stable over a long session:** 36 switches over the same 10 minutes.
- **Numbers and questions:** ~30 FLEURS sentences with digits, plus the 6 with ՞.

**Needs you (about 20 minutes total, can be done after my runs):**
1. **Names (Երևան, Գյումրի) and spoken numbers:** `python eval/record.py --speaker me` reads `eval/sentences.tsv`. Sentences s09, s15, s23 and s24 cover the names and numbers; s01, s05, s08 and s12 are questions.
2. **Spontaneous fast speech:** talk freely for 2 minutes with `python -m livetr --no-system`, then tell me what came out wrong.
3. **Real idle room:** run `python -m livetr --no-system` for 5 minutes with nobody speaking, and count the lines.
4. **Similar voices:** a short call with two people of the same gender on the call side. FLEURS can't test this, because it has only one man and two women.

I skipped the optional "translate with the previous sentence" card. It makes translation input longer, translation is already the slowest stage, and FLEURS sentences aren't connected, so I can't measure a gain before the meeting.

  split we use is held out). Spontaneous speech in calls is harder; see the live tests in DECISIONS.md.
- Rejected without benchmarking: small/tiny Armenian Whisper fine-tunes (published WER 40–78 %); cloud ASR
  (not allowed).

### Translation: NLLB-200 distilled 1.3B, CTranslate2 int8, on CPU
Supports `hye_Armn → eng_Latn`, 1.4 GB in int8. Compared with the 600M version on 150 FLEURS sentences against the
official English translations (`eval/run_mt_fleurs.py`):

| model | chrF | time per sentence mean / max | colloquial `ինչպես ես ինչ կա, չկա` |
|---|---|---|---|
| **NLLB 1.3B** | **63.5** | 1.81 / 4.68 s | "Hi, how are you?" |
| NLLB 600M | 60.1 | 1.00 / 2.59 s | "Hello, as I am, there is no such thing." |

Translation was the weakest stage, so we took the better model. In the whole program (test call, 15 s phrases) the
latency is p50 3.0 s, max 4.0 s, still under 5 s, and the program needs 3.6 GB instead of 2.8 GB. The margin is
smaller than with 600M, which stays the fallback: `--nllb-dir models/nllb-200-distilled-600M-ct2-int8`.
ASR and translation run on the CPU one after the other in the same worker, so they do not compete.

| Question from the brief | Result | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Is the Armenian recognised? | Yes on clear speech: CER 3.3 % / WER 10 % on single FLEURS sentences, 4.1 % / 10.2 % on a test call with long turns | `eval/run_asr.py`, `eval/score_call.py` |
| Is the English understandable? | Mostly. Translation errors follow ASR errors: `Քաղաքակրթություն` (civilization) heard as `Քաղաքականություն` (politics) gave "The word politics comes from the Latin syphilis" | `eval/results/live-runs/2026-10-23-test-call-live-blackhole.md` |
| Under 5 seconds? | Yes: with the final settings (15 s limit, NLLB 1.3B) p50 3.0 s, max 4.0 s on the test call; with NLLB 600M max 2.8 s; 0.8–1.5 s on the microphone (600M) | `--stats` |
| Are speakers kept apart? | 3 of 3 voices, 9/9 turns, also through the Opus codec calls use | `eval/score_call.py` |
| Local only? | Yes, runs with all network blocked | DECISIONS.md |

What does not work well, or is not proven yet:
- **Spontaneous, fast speech** is much harder than read speech. A YouTube conversation (with an older model)
  gave many wrong words; the current model has not yet been tested on such speech.
- **Very long turns are cut**, sometimes inside a word: each part is transcribed and translated on its own. With
  the first limit of 8 s, `քարանձավ` (cave) was split into "…մի։" and "Անցավ…", and the cave was missing from the
  English. Raising the limit to 15 s cut the test call 3 times instead of 11 and lowered CER from 5.7 % to 4.1 %,
  with latency still max 2.8 s. Turns over 15 s are still cut.
- **Two very similar voices** were never tested: the Armenian test data has only one man and two women.
- **Colloquial Armenian is hard to translate.** Live on the mic, ASR got spontaneous speech right (`էս`, `ա`), but
  NLLB 600M turned `ինչ կա, չկա` ("what's up") into "there is no such thing" and `չատերը` ("the chatbots") into
  "chessboard". NLLB 1.3B gets these mostly right ("Hi, how are you?", "chat"), but it was trained mostly on
  formal text, so slang will still go wrong.
- **Latency margin is about 1 s** with NLLB 1.3B (max 4.0 s). A call app running at the same time takes CPU; if
  phrases go over 5 s, switch to the 600M translator (`--nllb-dir`).
- **The Mac must be quiet**: with another heavy job running on battery, single phrases took up to 10 s.

## 7. Known limitations

"""Download every model once, so the program can run fully offline.

    python scripts/download_models.py                 # default ASR + NLLB + speaker + VAD
    python scripts/download_models.py --asr buzz mms  # extra ASR candidates for benchmarking
    python scripts/download_models.py --asr all
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import sys
from pathlib import Path

sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent))

from livetr.asr import MODELS  # noqa: E402
from livetr.cli import DEFAULT_ASR  # noqa: E402

NLLB_REPO = "facebook/nllb-200-distilled-1.3B"  # 600M is the fallback: --nllb-repo/--nllb-dir


def main() -> None:
    p = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    p.add_argument("--asr", nargs="+", default=[DEFAULT_ASR], help=f"keys from {list(MODELS)} or 'all'")
    p.add_argument("--nllb-repo", default=NLLB_REPO)
    p.add_argument("--nllb-dir", default="models/nllb-200-distilled-1.3B-ct2-int8")
    args = p.parse_args()
    keys = list(MODELS) if args.asr == ["all"] else args.asr

    from huggingface_hub import snapshot_download

    for key in keys:
        spec = MODELS[key]
        print(f"[asr] {key}: {spec.repo}")
        # MMS ships ~1,100 language adapters; fetch only the shared weights and the Armenian one.
        allow = None if spec.kind != "mms" else ["*.json", "*.txt", "model*.safetensors", f"adapter.{spec.language}.*"]

    from huggingface_hub import snapshot_download

    for key in keys:
        spec = MODELS[key]
        print(f"[asr] {key}: {spec.repo}")
        # MMS ships ~1,100 language adapters; fetch only the shared weights and the Armenian one.
        allow = None if spec.kind != "mms" else ["*.json", "*.txt", "model*.safetensors", f"adapter.{spec.language}.*"]
        snapshot_download(spec.repo, allow_patterns=allow)

    out = Path(args.nllb_dir)
    if (out / "model.bin").exists():
        print(f"[mt] {out} already exists")
    else:
        import ctranslate2
        from transformers import AutoTokenizer

        print(f"[mt] converting {args.nllb_repo} to CTranslate2 int8 -> {out}")
        # float16 + low_cpu_mem_usage: the 1.3B model must convert within 8 GB of RAM
        ctranslate2.converters.TransformersConverter(args.nllb_repo, load_as_float16=True, low_cpu_mem_usage=True).convert(
            str(out), quantization="int8")
        AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(args.nllb_repo).save_pretrained(str(out))

    print("[spk] speechbrain/spkrec-ecapa-voxceleb")
    from livetr.speakers import EcapaEmbedder

    EcapaEmbedder()

    print("[vad] silero (ships inside the pip package)")
    from livetr.segmenter import SileroVAD

    SileroVAD()
    print("done. The program now runs offline.")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

### Assistant · 2026-10-24 21:24:21

The timing tests are done, and they changed one setting. The program now translates with the smaller NLLB 600M model instead of 1.3B. With 1.3B, 4 of 23 lines went over 5 s when both sides talked at once. All 35 tests pass; there are four new commits on `main`, not pushed yet.

**Results by card:**

| Card | Result |
|---|---|
| Cut long turns at the quietest moment | ✅ Done. Fewer words split: CER 5.7% → 5.1% with an 8 s limit. |
| Both sides talking at once | ✅ With 600M: 0 of 23 lines over 5 s, max 3.5 s; the queue wait shows on every line. |
| Labels stable over a long session | ✅ 10.5 minutes, 36 turns: all labelled right, 3 of 3 speakers. |
| Warm-up and start-up time | ✅ 15 s from command to listening; first phrase 2.6 s. |
| 10 minutes without swap | ⚠️ Peak memory 2.1 GB, swap grew only ~0.2 GB. Not yet tested with Zoom open. |
| Silence and noise | ⚠️ Synthetic noise: 0 invented lines in 5 minutes. A real idle room still needs testing. |
| Numbers, names and questions | ⚠️ Numbers come out as Armenian words and read fine, but English gets the exact number in only 12/30; years are the weak spot. Names and questions need your recordings. |
| Spontaneous speech, similar voices | ⏳ Needs you. |
| Context translation (optional) | Skipped: it would slow the slowest stage, and I can't measure a gain in time. |

**About the model switch:** it was the rule already written in DECISIONS.md. The price is somewhat weaker translation of colloquial phrases (FLEURS chrF 60.1 instead of 63.5). If you want the better model for a call where mostly one side talks, add `--nllb-dir models/nllb-200-distilled-1.3B-ct2-int8`.

**Also fixed:** the model download script would have crashed, because it still referenced a field from the removed ASR models. It runs cleanly now.

**What you still need to do (about 20 minutes):**
1. `python eval/record.py --speaker me`, then tell me when it's done. This covers names, spoken numbers and questions.
2. `python -m livetr --no-system`: 2 minutes of free, fast talking, then 5 minutes of silence in the room.
3. A rehearsal Zoom call with two people of the same gender on the call side. Keep Activity Monitor open and watch Swap Used.

On meeting day, quit Ollama and your lawyer app first; Ollama restarts on its own, so check it's really gone. Want me to push the commits to GitHub?

**Tool result:**

```
def main() -> None:
    os.environ.setdefault("HF_HUB_OFFLINE", "1")
    p = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    p.add_argument("--nllb-dir", required=True)
    p.add_argument("--n", type=int, default=150)
    args = p.parse_args()
    from sacrebleu.metrics import CHRF

    model = Path(args.nllb_dir).name
    pairs = fleurs_pairs(args.n)
    tr = NllbTranslator(args.nllb_dir)
    tr.translate("Բարև")  # warm-up
    hyps, lats = [], []
    for hy, _ in pairs:
        t = time.monotonic()
        hyps.append(tr.translate(hy))
        lats.append(time.monotonic() - t)
    chrf = CHRF().corpus_score(hyps, [[en for _, en in pairs]]).score

    RESULTS.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    with open(RESULTS / f"mt_{model}.tsv", "w", encoding="utf-8", newline="") as f:
        w = csv.writer(f, delimiter="\t")
        w.writerow(["hy", "reference_en", "output_en", "seconds"])
        for (hy, en), out, lat in zip(pairs, hyps, lats):
            w.writerow([hy, en, out, f"{lat:.2f}"])
    lats.sort()
    row = dict(model=model, sentences=len(pairs), chrf=f"{chrf:.1f}", lat_mean=f"{sum(lats) / len(lats):.2f}",
               lat_p90=f"{lats[int(len(lats) * 0.9)]:.2f}", lat_max=f"{lats[-1]:.2f}", date=time.strftime("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M"))
    summary = RESULTS / "mt_results.csv"
    new = not summary.exists()
    with open(summary, "a", newline="") as f:
        w = csv.DictWriter(f, fieldnames=list(row))
        if new:
            w.writeheader()
        w.writerow(row)
    print("  ".join(f"{k}={v}" for k, v in row.items()))

    print("\ncolloquial phrases (live mic test):")
    with open(ROOT / "eval" / "colloquial.tsv", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        for r in csv.DictReader(f, delimiter="\t"):
            print(f"  HY: {r['hy']}\n  MT: {tr.translate(r['hy'])}\n  meant: {r['meaning']}\n")


if __name__ == "__main__":
