**While the phone is talking or playing in the call, do you hear it in your Mac headphones?**

- **No, I hear nothing:** the phone isn't sending sound into the call. It's probably muted in Meet, or its echo cancelling removes its own video. The Mac side is fine. Fix: unmute the phone in Meet, and *speak* into it from another room instead of playing a video on it.
- **Yes, I hear it, but there are no `Speaker` lines:** Meet's sound bypasses the Multi-Output Device. Fix: in Meet, ⚙ → Audio → Speakers → choose the Multi-Output Device again, then **reload the Meet tab** (Cmd+R). Browsers sometimes keep the old output until a reload.

**The cleanest test needs no phone at all:** press Ctrl-C, run `python -m livetr --meter`, and within 15 seconds click **Test** next to Speakers in Meet's Audio settings. If the **system** bar jumps, Meet → BlackHole works.

### Assistant · 2026-10-25 18:37:12

If you hear nothing, not even Meet's test sound, then Meet's sound isn't reaching the Multi-Output Device at all. The phone isn't the problem. Three quick checks, in this order, find where it stops:

**1. Does the Mac play sound through the Multi-Output Device at all?**
- Open a YouTube video in **another tab** (not Meet) and press play.
- You should hear it in your headphones, as in the rehearsal earlier.
- If you hear nothing: System Settings → Sound → Output → **Multi-Output Device**. In Audio MIDI Setup, also check the **External Headphones volume slider** in the Multi-Output Device; the volume keys don't work on it.

**2. Does Meet make any sound at all?**
- In Meet: ⚙ → Audio → Speakers → choose **External Headphones** (not Multi-Output) and click **Test**.
- **If you hear it:** Meet works, and the problem is only Meet + Multi-Output. Go to step 3.
- **If you still hear nothing:** the **tab may be muted**. Right-click the Meet tab and look for "Unmute site", or check for a crossed-out speaker icon on the tab.

**3. Meet + Multi-Output:**
- Back in Speakers, choose the entry that says **Default** (or "System default") instead of picking the Multi-Output Device by name. Meet then follows the system output, which is the Multi-Output Device from step 1.
- **Reload the Meet tab** (Cmd+R), then click **Test** again.
- You should hear it, and with `python -m livetr --meter` running, the **system** bar should jump.

Tell me where it stops: in step 1, 2 or 3.

### Assistant · 2026-10-25 18:37:49

That's expected, and it doesn't mean the Mac setup is broken. **A video playing on the phone itself is never sent into the call.** The phone keeps other apps' sound out of the call, and its echo cancelling removes whatever its own speaker plays. So the Mac receives nothing, whatever the settings are.

**Do one of these instead:**

**A. Meet's test sound (no phone needed, 10 seconds):**
1. In the terminal: `python -m livetr --meter`
2. In Meet: ⚙ → Audio → next to Speakers, click **Test**.
3. You should hear a short sound in the headphones, and the **system** bar should jump.

**B. Your voice through the phone:**
1. On the phone, open the Meet app and make sure the phone's mic is **unmuted** in the call.
2. Take the phone to another room and **speak** Armenian into it, or ask someone at home to say a few sentences.
3. On the Mac you should hear that voice in your headphones, and with `python -m livetr` running it should appear as `Speaker 1`.

**C. A video from a second device:** play the YouTube video on another laptop or a TV and hold the phone near it. The phone only cancels its own sound, so this gets through.

Option A is the quickest and needs nothing else. If the test sound plays in your headphones and the system bar jumps, the Meet setup is proven, and you're ready for the interview.

### Assistant · 2026-10-25 18:42:21

This run proves the Meet path works. **13 lines came in as `Speaker`**, so sound from the call reaches BlackHole and the program, with latency under 1 s. Meet → Multi-Output Device → BlackHole is confirmed.

What came through are only short fragments («Սից», «Սիր», «Հովհ.»). That's the phone's own YouTube sound, chopped up by its echo cancelling, as expected. Real people talking into the call will come through whole.

One thing in this output is worth fixing. **«Հովհ.» showed up 8 times as a call line, translated as "John: What is it?"**, and it did the same twice on the mic in the rehearsal. It's the ASR inventing text from noise: «Հովհ.» is the abbreviation of Հովհաննես used in Bible verse references, and the model's training data includes audiobooks. I'll check how often it appears in all outputs and whether it ever came from real speech.

"""Export local Claude Code session logs (~/.claude/projects/<this project>/*.jsonl) to Markdown in chats/.

    python scripts/export_chats.py            # one .md per session, oldest first

Everything in the conversation is written out unedited: user messages, assistant text, thinking, every tool call
with its full input, and every tool result. Only the logger's internal bookkeeping records (API request shapes,
cost state, file snapshots) are skipped. Re-run at the end so the current session is complete.
Sessions that ran on claude.ai (web) are not on this Mac; export those from claude.ai.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import datetime as dt
import json
import re
from pathlib import Path

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
LOGS = Path.home() / ".claude" / "projects" / re.sub(r"[^A-Za-z0-9]", "-", str(ROOT))
OUT = ROOT / "chats"


def fence(text: str, lang: str = "") -> str:
    ticks = "```"
    while ticks in text:
        ticks += "`"
    return f"{ticks}{lang}\n{text}\n{ticks}"


def result_text(content) -> str:
    if isinstance(content, str):
        return content
    parts = []
    for b in content or []:
        if b.get("type") == "text":
            parts.append(b["text"])
        else:
            parts.append(f"[{b.get('type')} block]")
    return "\n".join(parts)
